allows to compute the time derivative of
Au
in terms of
\partial_{t}u
and the adjoint operator
A^{*}
of
A
.

Lemma A.9

(Time derivative of
Au
,
[
GN21
, Lemma 3.7]
)
.

Let
V,\,W
be reflexive, separable Banach spaces and
Y,\,X
Hilbert spaces such that we have the Gelfand triples
V\xhookrightarrow{}Y\xhookrightarrow{}V^{\prime}
and
W\xhookrightarrow{}X\xhookrightarrow{}W^{\prime}
.
Moreover, assume that for a linear and bounded operator
A\in{\mathcal{L}}(Y,X)
for its adjoint
A^{*}
we have
A^{*}\rvert_{W}\in{\mathcal{L}}(W;V)
.
Then, for all
u\in L^{2}(0,T;Y)
with
\partial_{t}u\in L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})
there holds
\partial_{t}(Au)\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{\prime})
with

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}(Au),w\right\rangle_{W^{\prime},W}=\left\langle\partial_{t}u,A^{*}w\right\rangle_{V^{\prime},\,V}\qquad\text{ for all }w\in W.

(A.10)

Consequently, we have the estimate
\lVert\partial_{t}(Au)\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;W^{\prime})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\leq\lVert A^{*}\rvert_{W}\rVert_{\smash{{\mathcal{L}}(W;V)}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\lVert\partial_{t}u\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}
.

Proof.

The proof is based on the uniqueness of the generalised time derivative; we omit its details.
∎

As mentioned in
[
GN21
, p.1591]
, concerning the spatial regularity of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
we crucially need that the lateral channel boundary
N
of the channel
Z_{*}
does not touch the lateral boundary of the standard cell
Z
as otherwise one has to consider functions with vanishing trace on
N\cap\partial Z
.
Since we aim to obtain an estimate for
\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})
, where
\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(0,T;({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime})
, we restrict the domain of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
to functions vanishing on the top and bottom
S_{*}^{\pm}
of the channel in
Z
in what follows, although this assumption is not needed for the spatial regularity result stated below.

Lemma A.10

(Spatial regularity of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
)
.

The averaging operator
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
is a linear and bounded operator

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\colon L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))\to L^{2}(0,T;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}}).

(A.11)

Moreover, for all
\varphi\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))
we have
\nabla_{x}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}(\nabla_{z}\varphi)
and the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\leq\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}.

(A.12)

Proof.

Following the arguments given in
[
GNK18
, Proposition 6]
, for
\varphi\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))
and
v_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})
, we calculate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi)(t,x)\partial_{x_{i}}v_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{Z_{*}}\varphi(t,x^{\prime},z){\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x_{i}}v_{\varepsilon})(t,x^{\prime},z)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

(A.13)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{Z_{*}}\partial_{z_{i}}\varphi(t,x^{\prime},z)({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})(t,x^{\prime},z)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

(A.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\partial Z_{*}}\varphi(t,x^{\prime},z)({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})(t,x^{\prime},z)\nu_{i}(z)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{H}}^{n-1}(z)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(A.15)

where we have used
Lemma A.3

(iii)
and integrated by parts in the last step. By the choice of
v_{\varepsilon}
, we have
{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rvert_{\partial Z_{*}}=0
; thus, the boundary integral vanishes and the weak differentiability of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi
is proven. Consequently, for every
\varphi\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))
we calculate

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\lVert{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\lVert{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}(\nabla_{z}\varphi)\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}^{2}\leq\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}},

(A.16)

where we have used
Corollary A.8
for the last estimate.
∎

To apply
Lemma A.9
to the unfolded sequence
({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
, we consider
{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
as stationary operators, which is possible due to their definition in which time only acts as an additional parameter.

Corollary A.11

(Estimate for
\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})
,
[
GN21
, Proposition 3.10]
)
.

Let
v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})
with
\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(0,T;({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime})
.
Then,
\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Sigma,H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))^{\prime})
and, for all
\varphi\in L^{2}(\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))
and almost every
t\in(0,T)
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}),\varphi\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\Sigma;(H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))^{\prime}),\,L^{2}(\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\varphi\right\rangle_{({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime},\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}}}.

(A.17)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Sigma;(H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))^{\prime}))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\lVert\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}.

(A.18)

Proof.

In view of
Lemma A.9
, we set

\displaystyle V={\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}},\qquad Y=L^{2}(\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon}),\qquad W=L^{2}(\Sigma;H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*})),\qquad X=L^{2}(\Sigma\times Z_{*})

(A.19)

and
A={\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{L}}(Y;X)
with
A^{*}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
. Then, by
Lemma A.10
, we have that the restriction
A^{*}\rvert_{W}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}\rvert_{W}\in{\mathcal{L}}(W;V)
is well-defined. Consequently,
\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Sigma;(H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*}))^{\prime}))
and (
A.17
) holds. The bound (
A.18
) is then a direct consequence of the estimates in
Lemma A.9
and
Lemma A.10
.
∎

The second tool for the application of the Kolmogorov-type compactness result from
[
GN16
]
is a good control of shifts with respect to the
x^{\prime}
-variable.
First, we show that shifts of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}
with respect to
x^{\prime}\in\Sigma
are estimated by shifts of
v_{\varepsilon}
into direction of
(N^{\prime},0)\varepsilon
with
N^{\prime}\in{\mathbbm{Z}}^{n-1}
.

Lemma A.12

(Shifts of the unfolded function,
[
GN21
, Lemma 3.11]
)
.

For
h\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, there exist
\varepsilon_{0},\xi_{0}>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\ll\varepsilon_{0}
,
\xi^{\prime}\in{\mathbbm{R}}^{n-1}
with
|\xi^{\prime}|\ll\xi_{0}
and
v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})
there holds

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi^{\prime},\cdot)-{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Sigma_{2h}\times Z_{*})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n-1}}\lVert\delta_{l^{\prime},\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\widehat{\Omega}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon,h})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}^{2},

(A.20)

where
l^{\prime}=l^{\prime}(\varepsilon,\xi^{\prime},m^{\prime})=m^{\prime}+\left\lfloor\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{\varepsilon}\right\rfloor\in{\mathbbm{Z}}^{n-1}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the ideas in
[
NJ07
, p. 709]
by introducing a special decomposition of each microcell
\varepsilon(Y+k^{\prime})
, which allows to estimate
\left\lfloor\frac{x^{\prime}+\xi^{\prime}}{\varepsilon}\right\rfloor
for
x^{\prime}\in\varepsilon(Y+k^{\prime})
, and uses
\Sigma_{2h}\subset\widehat{\Sigma}_{\varepsilon,h}
for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small.
∎

The main result for strong two-scale compactness is the following. Its proof is similar to that of
[
GN21
, Theorem 3.12]
, but we give some details for the main steps for the convenience of the reader. A similar result is established in
[
Ama+10
, Lemma 4.2]
but based on
L^{\infty}
-estimates for the sequence
(v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
, which however we cannot guarantee in our setting.

Theorem A.13

(Strong two-scale compactness)
.

Let
p\in[1,2)
,
\beta\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon}))
with
\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(0,T;({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime})
such that

i)

we have the estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\lVert\partial_{t}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}(0,T;({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon,0}^{\mathrm{M}})^{\prime})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lVert\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\lesssim 1,

(A.21)

ii)

for all
h\in(0,1)
sufficiently small and
l^{\prime}\in{\mathbbm{Z}}^{n-1}
with
|l^{\prime}\varepsilon|\ll h
the following convergence holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lVert\delta_{l^{\prime},\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\widehat{\Omega}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon,h})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\lVert\delta_{l^{\prime},\varepsilon}\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{2}((0,T)\times\widehat{\Omega}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*,\varepsilon,h})}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon l^{\prime}\to 0}0.

(A.22)

Then, there exists
v_{0}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Sigma;H^{1}(Z_{*})))
such that, at least for a subsequence, the following convergences hold:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rclcl}v_{\varepsilon}&\to&v_{0}&&\text{in the two-scale sense},\\
\varepsilon\nabla v_{\varepsilon}&\to&\nabla_{z}v_{0}&&\text{in the two-scale sense},\\
{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}&\to&v_{0}&&\text{strongly in }L^{p}(\Sigma;L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*}))).\end{array}

Proof.

The two-scale convergence of
(v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
and
(\varepsilon\nabla v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
follows directly from
Lemma A.1

(iii)
. In order to establish the strong convergence of the sequence

\displaystyle({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}\subset L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Sigma;H^{1}(Z_{*})))\xhookrightarrow{}L^{p}(\Sigma;L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*}))),

(A.26)

we use the Kolmogorov-type compactness result given in
[
GN16
, Theorem 2.2]
and have to check the following two conditions:

(K1)

For every rectangle
C\subset\Sigma
, the sequence
(\int_{C}{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x^{\prime})_{\varepsilon}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*}))
.

(K2)

For every
\xi^{\prime}\in{\mathbbm{R}}^{n-1}
with
0\leq\xi_{i}^{\prime}\leq 1
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi^{\prime},\cdot)-{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{p}(\Sigma_{\xi^{\prime}};L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*})))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\xrightarrow{\xi^{\prime}\to 0}0,

(A.27)

where
\Sigma_{\xi^{\prime}}=\Sigma\cap(\Sigma-\xi^{\prime})
.
By the compactness of the embedding
H^{1}(Z_{*})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{\beta}(Z_{*})
and the continuity of
H^{\beta}(Z_{*})\xhookrightarrow{}H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*})^{\prime}
, due to the Aubin-Lions lemma it suffices to show the boundedness of
\left(\int_{C}{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\right)_{\varepsilon}
in
W^{1,2,2}(0,T;H^{1}(Z_{*}),H^{1}_{\pm,0}(Z_{*})^{\prime})
in order to prove
(K1)
. This follows the same arguments as in
[
GN21
, Theorem 3.12]
, using
i)
,
Lemma A.3
and the norm estimate on
\partial_{t}({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})
from
Corollary A.11
.

For the proof of
(K2)
, by the triangle inequality, using
\Sigma_{\xi^{\prime}}\setminus(\Sigma_{\xi^{\prime}})_{h}\subset\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{h}
, it suffices to show that for
h>0
and
\xi\leq\xi_{0}(h)
sufficiently small we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi^{\prime},\cdot)-{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{p}(\Sigma_{h};L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*})))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\xi^{\prime}\to 0}0,

(A.28a)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{p}(\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{h};L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*})))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{h\to 0}0.

(A.28b)

The convergence (
A.28b
) is immediate by Hölder’s inequality using
i)
and
|\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{h}|\lesssim|h|
. To establish (
A.28a
), one may use
Lemma A.12
together with
ii)
to obtain for given
\rho>0
and
|\xi^{\prime}|\leq\xi_{0}(h)
and
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}(h)
sufficiently small

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}(h)]}\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi^{\prime},\cdot)-{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{p}(\Sigma_{h};L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*})))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\leq\frac{\rho}{2}.

(A.29)

Finally, as
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\in{\mathbbm{N}}
, there are only finitely many values of
\varepsilon>\varepsilon_{0}(h)
, and applying the standard Kolomogorov-argument to the finite family
({\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(h)}
, for
|\xi^{\prime}|
possibly being even smaller, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>\varepsilon_{0}(h)}\lVert{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi^{\prime},\cdot)-{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon}v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\smash{L^{p}(\Sigma_{h};L^{2}(0,T;H^{\beta}(Z_{*})))}\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{\mathstrut}}\leq\frac{\rho}{2},

(A.30)

which concludes the proof of (
A.28a
).
∎
